実質ＧＤＰ成長率の推移(年度）(前年比）
(資料）内閣府経済社会総合研究所「四半期別ＧＤＰ速報」
(年度）
予測

＜実質成長率：2026 年度 0.9％、2027 年度 1.3％を予想＞

１. 2026 年 4-6 月期の実質 GDP（2 次速報値）は、設備投資の上方修正などから、1 次速報
の前期比 0.3％（年率 1.1％）から前期比 0.4％（年率 1.4％）に上方修正された。 
２. GDP2 次速報の結果を受けて、8 月に発表した経済見通しを改定した。実質 GDP 成長率
は 2026 年度が 0.9％、2027 年度が 1.3％と予想する。2026 年 4-6 月期の実績値が上振れ
たことを受けて、2026 年度の見通しを 0.1％上方修正した。 
３. 景気は、物価上昇ペースの加速に伴う実質購買力の低下を背景に民間消費が低迷するこ
とを主因に、2026 年度末にかけて停滞色が強まるだろう。 
４. 2027 年度に入ると、飲食料品の消費税率引き下げに伴う物価上昇率の低下から家計の実
質購買力が改善し、民間消費が回復に向かうほか、原材料価格の低下に伴う企業収益の
改善を受けて設備投資の増勢ペースが加速することが見込まれる。 
５. 消費者物価上昇率（生鮮食品を除く総合）は、輸入物価の大幅上昇が食料品や日用品等
の幅広い品目に波及し、2026 年度末には 3％台まで加速するが、飲食料品の消費税率引
き下げを受けて、2027 年度には 1％台まで鈍化するだろう。消費者物価上昇率（生鮮食
品を除く総合）は、2026 年度が 2.3％、2027 年度が 1.3％と予想する。

2026・2027 年度経済見通し 
－26 年 4-6 月期ＧＤＰ2 次速報後改定

経済研究部 経済調査部長 斎藤  太郎 
(03)3512-1836  tsaito@nli-research.co.jp 
ニッセイ基礎研究所 2026-09-08

### Page 2

2026 年 4-6 月期の GDP 統計は、複数の特殊要因（高校授業料・小学校給食費の無償化1、特許権
の海外譲渡2、国家備蓄原油の放出3）が需要項目を大きく変動させており、個々の需要項目から景
気の基調を読み取りにくい結果となった。 ただし、 中東情勢の悪化による下押し圧力を受けながら
も、 実質GDP が 0％台半ばから後半とされる潜在成長率を上回る伸びを確保したことは、 景気の底
堅さを示すものとして一定の評価ができるだろう。

（経常利益は過去最高の更新が続く） 
財務省の法人企業統計によれば、2026 年 4-6 月期の全産業（金融業、保険業を除く、以下同
じ）の経常利益は前年比 24.6％（1-3 月期：同 14.6％）と 7 四半期連続の増益となり、前期から伸
びが高まった。非製造業が前年比 9.8％（1-3 月期：同 1.4％）と 22 四半期連続の増加、製造業が
前年比 57.0％（1-3 月期：同 42.9％）と 4 四半期連続の増加となり、いずれも増加ペースが前期か
ら加速した。 
季節調整済の経常利益は前期比 11.0％（1-3 月期：同 5.9％）と 4 四半期連続で増加した。製造
業（前期比 12.1％） 、非製造業（同10.2％）ともに前期比二桁の高い伸びとなった。製造業は 4 四
半期連続で前期比二桁の伸びとなった。 
経常利益（季節調整値）は 37.7 兆円（1-3 月
期：34.0 兆円）と 4 四半期連続で過去最高水
準を更新した。製造業、非製造業ともに過去
最高となった。 
2026 年 4-6 月期は、中東情勢の悪化を受け
たエネルギーの供給制約、物流の停滞、原油
価格高騰等が企業収益を下押しすることが懸
念されていた。しかし、実際には原油価格上
昇の影響で製造業、非製造業ともに変動費は

1 家計消費から政府消費への振り替えが生じたため、家計消費が押し下げられる一方、政府消費が押し上げられた。 
2 サービス輸出を押し上げる一方、設備投資（研究開発）を押し下げた。 
3 原油輸入量の急減が成長率を押し上げる一方、原油の調達難を受けた国家備蓄原油の放出により、公的在庫変動が大
幅なマイナス寄与となった。

### Page 3

（実質賃金上昇率は 2026 年度末にかけていったんマイナスへ） 
名目賃金を消費者物価 （持家の帰属家賃を除く総合） で割り引いた実質賃金は、2026 年 1 月に 13
ヵ月ぶりのプラスとなった後、7 ヵ月連続でプラスの伸びを維持している。名目賃金（現金給与総
額）が 2025 年中の 2％台前半から 2026 年入り後には 3％台へ伸びを高めたことに加え、政府によ
るエネルギー負担緩和策の影響などから、消費者物価（持家の帰属家賃を除く総合）の上昇率が大
きく縮小したことが実質賃金の押し上げに寄与している。 
実質賃金上昇率は、 消費者物価の上昇ペース加速を受けて伸び率が徐々に鈍化し、2026 年度末に
かけていったんマイナスに転じるだろう。 しかし、2027 年度入り後には、飲食料品の消費税率引き
下げを受けて消費者物価上昇率 が鈍化することを主因として 再びプラスに転じることが予想され
る。

２. 実質成長率は 2026 年度 0.9％、2027 年度 1.3％を予想 
2026 年 4-6 月期の GDP2 次速報を受けて、8/18 に発表した経済見通しを改定した。実質 GDP 成
長率は 2026 年度が 0.9％、2027 年度が 1.3％と予想する。2026 年 4-6 月期の実績値が方修正された
がことを受けて、2026 年度の見通しを 0.1％上方修正した。

日本経済は、2025 年 10-12 月期から 2026 年 4-6 月期まで 3 四半期連続で 0％台半ばから後半とさ
れる潜在成長率を上回る成長となったが、 先行きは回復ペースが鈍化することが予想される。 消費
者物価の川上段階に当たる企業物価はすでに大きく上昇しており、 先行きは食料品や日用品など幅
広い品目で値上げの動きが広がり、 消費者物価の上昇ペースは再加速することが見込まれる。 物価
上昇に伴う実質購買力の低下を主因として民間消費は低迷が続き、2027 年 1-3 月期は飲食料品の消

### Page 4

（物価の見通し） 
消費者物価（生鮮食品を除く総合、以下コアCPI）は、2026 年 1 月に前年比 1.9％と 2022 年 3 月
以来、3 年 10 ヵ月ぶりの 2％割れとなった後、7 月まで 7 ヵ月連続で 1％台の伸びとなっている。
消費者物価上昇率鈍化の理由は、既往の原油価格下落に加え、ガソリンの暫定税率廃止、電気・ガ
ス代の支援策などからエネルギー価格が下落している
こと、2025 年夏場以降、食料の上昇率鈍化が続いてい
ることである。 
ただし、 消費者物価の川上段階に位置する飲食料品の
輸入物価はすでに上昇率が高まっており、 今後、 川下の
消費者物価の食料品に波及することが見込まれる。 
日本銀行の 「企業物価指数」 によれば、 中東情勢の影
響を受けた原油価格の上昇や円安の進展を反映し、 輸入

### Page 2

2  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/
上週重要市場資訊
⚫ 上周三美國公布 8 月民間就業人數僅增加 3.8 萬人，低於市場預期的 4.8 萬人。
教育與醫療服務業新增 4.5 萬人，但製造業減少 1.7 萬人，專業與商業服務業
也出現萎縮 。 這反映出民間部門招聘動能放緩 ， 尤其是製造業與專業服務業的疲
弱。美元指數至高點回落 ， 惟上周五美國公布8 月非農就業人數新增 16.2 萬人 ，
遠超預期的 5.6 萬人 ，失業率維持在4.1%；勞動參與率回升至 61.6%， 平均工時
延長至 34.4 小時 ， 顯示勞動市場的韌性 。 雖然薪資增幅僅3.1%， 通膨壓力有限 ，
但強勁的就業數據仍強化了市場對美聯儲加息的預期 。 芝商所 FedWatch 工具顯
示，9 月加息機率已升至 62%，美元指數止跌回穩。
⚫ 紐西蘭央行上週三將官方隔夜拆款利率調升 25bps 至 2.75%， 並釋出緩步緊縮訊
號。央行聲明指出，逐步撤回貨幣政策刺激有助於通膨回到 2%目標中點，同時

降低未來大幅升息的必要性 。 總裁佈雷曼強調 ， 升息時機仍高度不確定 ， 短期核
心通膨可能續升 ， 但逐步退出寬鬆政策仍屬合宜 。 央行亦提醒經濟存在顯著下行
風險，政策將保持漸；然而消息公布後紐幣大跌，主因 2027 年利率預測較原先
預期低。
⚫ 加拿大央行上週三維持政策利率在 2.25%不變 ， 但警告通膨風險升高 。 美國新關
稅增加經濟不確定性 ， 或使企業延後投資與招聘 。 總裁麥克勒姆表示 ， 關稅對整
體經濟影響有限 ， 但中東戰事與高油價可能推升物價壓力 。 央行強調需警惕外部
因素對通膨的擴散效應，加拿大幣止跌回穩。

### Page 3

3  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/
美元兌新台幣匯率走勢
Sep/7/2026
＜上週走勢＞
上週台幣匯價高點為 31.590，低點在 31.827，以 31.630 作收，較前一週收盤價
貶值 0.2 分。
上週台幣先貶後升，週初，聯準會主席華許於全球央行年會發表的鷹派言論強
化市場對聯準會升息的預期，引發台灣市場股匯雙殺，外資匯出令台幣走貶；週二
早盤由於台股走升外資匯入，台幣一度走升，惟逢低進口商買美金力道強勁，加以
投信亦匯出，台幣由升轉貶，不過逢高出口商拋匯，台幣貶幅收斂；週三、周四，
中東戰事升溫，帶動美元指數走升，外資連續兩日賣超台股共計1390 億，匯出力道
強勁，台幣貶至周內低點 31.827，惟出口商拋匯需求及央行進場調節，抑制台幣貶
值空間；臨近周末，受益於美元指數走跌、亞幣走升，台幣止跌回穩，加以外資轉
向匯入，台幣收復上周大部分跌幅，終場在央行調節之下收在 31.630。
＜未來分析＞
預估本週台幣區間震盪。就國際市場而言，美國勞動力市場尚屬穩健，非農就

業人數遠超預期，勞動參與率回升，令市場對於聯準會升息的預期有所回溫，將給
予美元指數支撐；就台灣市場來看，台灣 8 月製造業 PMI 54.7，顯示台灣製造業景
氣續呈大幅擴張，新增訂單持續暢旺，有利台幣表現，惟上週外資賣超台股並於外
匯市場匯出，將為台幣帶來貶值的壓力。綜合以上，預估本週台幣走勢區間震盪。
＜預估本週＞ 美元兌台幣：31.500-31.850

央加速升息的預期下急跌，然匯價於 155 整數關卡止跌，若能有效跌破則可能還有

＜上週走勢＞ 
    上週 EUR/USD 震盪走升。週初，歐元跌深反彈，不過在美伊不確定性再次升
溫及美國利率維持偏高路徑的定價支撐美元需求下，EUR/USD 弱勢整理；週間，市
場在等待美國就業相關數據公布，歐元持續走落，最低來到 1.1565；臨近週末，美
國就業數據好於預期，但聯準會官員鴿派言論，加上川普發言使升息預期受抑制，
帶動歐元先下後上，終場收在 1.1613，週線上漲 33 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 EUR/USD 區間震盪，主要原因如下：一、美國 8 月非農就業增長超
預期，帶動交易員對聯準會本月重啟升息的押注顯著升溫。對此，美國總統川普上
週五強烈抨擊高利率使美國陷入劣勢，揚言若聯準會不調降借貸成本，美國將直接
停止與對美享有貿易順差的國家進行貿易 ， 意圖以極端貿易禁令迫使央行寬鬆。二、
受能源推動，歐元區 8 月通膨升至 3.3%，但核心通膨降至 2.4%，顯示油價上漲未
出現進一步滲透至工資、終端服務定價等長期通膨預期的二輪效應。歐洲央行管委
科赫示警通膨上行風險，強化 9 月升息預期，短線上為歐元提供支撐。三、技術面
而言 ， 在日線圖中 ，EUR/USD 匯價在多次向下測試 1.1550 的關鍵價位均獲得支撐 ，
目前承壓於年線但獲得月線支撐。綜合以上，預估 EUR/USD 本週呈現區間震盪的
可能性較高。

Document 050: 25年来罕见！10年期美债收益率反超标普500盈利收益率

Source PDF: all report pdf/25年来罕见！10年期美债收益率反超标普500盈利收益率.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-09-28
Pages: 3
Status: ok

### Page 1

10 年期美国国债收益率突破 5% ，债券相对股票的吸引⼒攀升⾄ 25 年来最⾼。以标普 500 指
数市盈率倒数衡量的股票盈利收益率，⽬前已低于 10 年期美国国债收益率，债券在收益层
⾯对股市形成明显压制。 Shiller 模型警⽰，未来⼗年标普 500 每年跑赢债券或仅约 1% 。 20
年来股优于债的投资范式正式终结。
摘要：
债券与股票之间的收益率差已逆转⾄ 25 年以来最有利于债券的⽔平，这⼀结构性转变正迫使投
资者重新审视资产配置逻辑。
10 年期美国国债收益率突破 5% ，使债券相较股票的吸引⼒达到约 25 年来最⾼⽔平。以标普 500
指数市盈率倒数衡量的股票盈利收益率，⽬前已低于 10 年期美国国债收益率，这意味着债券在
收益层⾯已对股票形成明显压制。
这⼀格局对股市构成潜在下⾏压⼒。耶鲁⼤学经济学家 Robert Shiller 的周期调整超额 CAPE 收
益率模型显⽰，未来⼗年标普 500 指数每年跑赢债券的幅度可能仅为 1% 左右。投资者对企业盈
利预期的容错空间已⼤幅收窄。

⻓债投资者遭受重创，泡沫破裂痕迹明显
⾼收益率在某种程度上是经济韧性的体现 —— 美股接近历史⾼点，美国经济表现强于预期，⻓
端利率上⾏的冲击⽬前尚未在宏观层⾯充分显现。
然⽽，率先承压的是此前押注⻓期债券的投资者。以追踪 20 年期及以上美国国债的 TLT ETF 为
参照，这类投资者已遭受⼤幅亏损。
回望过去，危机后⼗年低通胀与政府⼲预共同催⽣的债券⽜市，以及疫情期间的价格急升，事
后来看颇具资产泡沫特征。
市场规律表明，泡沫破裂往往意味着买⼊时机的到来。当前 5% 以上的 10 年期国债收益率，是数
⼗年来罕⻅的⽔平，债券的配置价值已得到实质性重估。

中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

2 条评论
收益率倒挂逻辑终结，⼆⼗年股优于债的范式已被打破
过去的市场正统观念认为，股票因具备增⻓潜⼒，理应享有相对于债券的估值溢价，即股票盈
利收益率⾃然低于债券收益率。
然⽽，全球⾦融危机后⻓达约⼆⼗年的时间⾥，这⼀逻辑被彻底颠覆 —— 股票盈利收益率⻓期
⾼于债券收益率，令股票成为⽆可争议的优势资产。
如今，这⼀格局再度逆转。将股票盈利收益率与 10 年期美国国债收益率相⽐较，债券的相对吸
引⼒已回到约 25 年前的⽔平。
这意味着，即便股市市盈率已有所收缩以消化更⾼的债券收益率，股市前景仍⾯临⼀定的压
⼒。
Shiller 模型发出警⽰，但历史局限性同样值得关注
Robert Shiller 的超额 CAPE 收益率指标，通过⽐较经周期调整的实际盈利收益率与 10 年期国债

收益率，来预测股票未来⼗年的超额回报。
历史数据显⽰，该指标对未来⼗年股票超额收益具有显著的预测效⼒，⽽当前读数仅暗⽰标普
500 指数在未来⼗年中每年跑赢债券约 1% 。
不过，该模型近年来的预测精度有所下滑 —— 实际股市表现远超其预期，这或许与市场持续受
到政策⼲预有关。
尽管如此，核⼼信号仍然清晰：债券的替代价值已是⼀代⼈以来最⾼。在这⼀背景下，投资者
需要以更为严苛的标准审视标普 500 的盈利增⻓预测，因为⼀旦企业利润不及预期，债市将不再
提供任何缓冲。

Document 052: 260915_ms_MLCC

Source PDF: all report pdf/260915_ms_MLCC.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 43
Status: ok

### Page 2

M
Japan Insight
2
Investment Perspective
MLCC is early cycle in AI server & rack penetration
There is immense change underway for the MLCC industry with an investment narrative
that is changing as AI chips/server demand is increasingly driving MLCC scarcity.  AI is
shifting from semiconductors to MLCCs, creating a supply/demand mismatch in high-end
components. The high-end AI MLCC market is effectively a Japanese-Korean duopoly with
Murata and SEMCO together hold around 85% share. High-capacitance, low-ESL parts for

AI accelerators require hundreds to over a thousand dielectric layers with near-zero
defects and face strict customer qualification — the barrier preventing a repeat of 2017-
18, when shortages invited entry and the cycle self-corrected. AI platforms now compete
directly with consumer, automotive, and industrial applications for advanced materials,
heightening supply risk for MLCC traditional customers. We are still in the early innings,
and supply concerns are extending beyond near-term shortages to longer-term supply

security. As a result, we are seeing an increasing number of long-term supply agreements
being signed between suppliers and customers.
Exhibit 1: Sequencing the technology cycle – still early cycle for MLCC
Source:  Morgan Stanley Research estimates
Turning point in MLCC pricing
While Murata Mfg.’s CFO and Executive Deputy President Masanori Minamide stated in a
Sep 14 Nikkei interview that rising production loads for high-end products are making it

increasingly difficult to fully supply low-end products, and that the priority of defending
low-end market share has effectively declined, we believe the company’s fundamental
policy of not changing prices based solely on short-term supply-demand conditions
remains intact. That said, in our view, Murata has been gradually shifting its strategy
toward focusing more on its areas of strength and adopting pricing that better reflects the
need to cover large-scale capital investments, particularly in those core segments where it

enjoys a competitive advantage. We believe that Murata’s reluctance to enter into L TAs for
AI/data-center use value added MLCCs reflects its view that locking in prices today may
not be optimal, given the potential for MLCC pricing to rise in the future.
We expect sales growth to continue for small-sized, high-
capacity, high-value-added MLCCs for AI servers/DCs

### Page 3

M
Japan Insight
Morgan Stanley MUFG 3
MLCCs are a key business, accounting for 50% of sales at Murata, 46% at SEMCO, 19% at
Yageo, and 70% at Taiyo Yuden in 2025. We expect global MLCC shipment value to
expand at a CAGR of 20.3%, from $18.51bn in 2025 to $44.45bn in 2031. We believe
growth is set to accelerate from the 5.0% CAGR recorded in global MLCC shipment value
over the 13 years of 2012-25 because we expect continued growth in sales of small, high-

capacity, high-value-added MLCCs for AI servers/datacenters (DCs) from 2026. For AI
servers/DCs alone, we forecast MLCC shipment value to reach $23.33bn by 2031 (vs. our
previous estimate of $10.80bn), implying a 57.1% CAGR (vs. 38.1%).
Howard Kao (our Greater China Technology Hardware analyst) expects MLCC demand
volume for VR200 NVL72 racks to increase about 80% versus GB300 NVL72 racks, and
forecasts the number of MLCCs mounted in each Rubin NVL72 rack will jump to roughly

570,000 units from around 320,000 for GB300 NVL72 racks, with MLCC dollar content
per rack estimated to increase by 166%.　We expect the rapid expansion of the high-value-
added MLCC market for AI servers/DCs to be especially positive for  earnings at Murata (#1
market share in MLCCs for 2025 at 41%) and SEMCO (#2 at 23%).

Key roles of MLCCs in AI/DC
MLCCs play a role in locally stabilizing power supply (instantaneous current supply plus

noise removal) near semiconductors, such as GPUs, CPUs, and FPGAs. Since GPUs, CPUs,
and FPGAs repeatedly switch on and off on a nanosecond timescale, they demand large
currents instantaneously, and MLCCs meet this instantaneous current demand by
immediately discharging their stored charge. As semiconductor miniaturization advances,
circuit operating speed increases and drive voltage falls. As voltage decreases, even small
voltage fluctuations risk causing semiconductor malfunction, so even more stable power

supply voltage is required than before. Demand for capacitor capacitance is also increasing
in line with AI servers' rising computing power and power consumption, and because
board area and mountable space are limited, we expect demand for smaller, higher-
capacity MLCCs to persist.
Sharp dips vs. share price peaks provide re-entry opportunity
Year-to-date to Sep 11, share prices of the four companies had risen 127% for Murata,
449% for SEMCO, 141% for Yageo, and 160% for Taiyo Yuden. However, from their

respective June-July peaks, these have fallen 40% for Murata, 38% for SEMCO, 52% for

### Page 4

Yageo, and 59% for Taiyo Yuden. Considering the strong likelihood that high-value-added
products for AI servers/DCs will continue to drive growth in MLCC demand not only in
2026 but also from 2027 onward, and that each company's earnings will stay on an
expansionary trend, we believe the conditions are now in place to consider re-entering
these stocks.

Earnings and share price risks
We expect capital expenditure on AI/DC to keep expanding and generational transitions to

### Page 6

Japan Insight
6
continue in GPUs, sustaining growth in demand for small, high-capacity, high-value-added
MLCCs. Risks to the continuity of AI/DC capex expansion include: (1) rising risk from the
growing practice of hyperscalers and major semiconductor companies charging usage fees
in exchange for equity investments in AI development companies, conditional on the use
of their own cloud services or semiconductors; and (2) risk that AI/DC capex growth slows

due to bottlenecks, such as power infrastructure constraints. If the pace of AI/DC capex
growth slows, we cannot rule out that MLCC demand falls short of our base case outlook
for demand. Also, as shown in Exhibits Exhibit 8 Exhibit 8  and Exhibit 9 , during the IT
bubble around 2000, there was a strong correlation between the YoY change in Murata's
and Taiyo Yuden's monthly order intake and their market cap. Since order intake has risen
sharply since Jul-Sep 2025, we cannot ignore the possibility that YoY growth in order

intake will slow. On the other hand, MLCC demand continues to expand for high-value-
added products for AI/DC, and we expect the major MLCC makers' sales to grow along
with an improving product mix.

We expect global MLCC shipment value to increase at 20.3%
CAGR  through 2031
As shown in Exhibit 10 ,  we estimate that global MLCC shipment value fell for two
consecutive years, from $17.47bn in 2021 (up 26.0% YoY) to $14.32bn in 2022 (down
18.1%) and $12.64bn in 2023 (down 11.7%), before rising back up to $13.30bn in 2024 (up
5.2%) and $14.67bn in 2025 (up 10.3%). We increase our forecasts for shipment value from

$18.48bn to $18.51bn for 2026 (up 26.2% YoY), from $22.16bn to $24.52bn for 2027 (up
32.4%), and from $25.73bn to $28.60bn for 2028 (up 16.7%), and we expect the market to
reach $44.45bn in 2031, growing at a CAGR of 20.3% over the six-year period.

AI/DC MLCC market to continue growing at a rapid pace
We expect growth in demand to accelerate for high-value-added products for AI/DC from

the second half of 2026. Howard Kao  expects MLCC demand volume for VR200 NVL72
racks to increase about 80% versus GB300 NVL72 racks, and forecasts the number of
MLCCs mounted in each Rubin NVL72 rack will rise sharply to roughly 570,000 units from
around 320,000 units for GB300 NVL72 racks (Exhibit 11 ). This increase stems from a
rise in the number of MLCCs mounted on both compute boards and switch boards, as well
as an increase in PCB content across the overall system, including modules such as CX9

Orchid. Howard Kao estimates this increase in MLCC mounting volume will lift the dollar-
based MLCC content per rack by 166% ( Exhibit 12 ).

Role of MLCCs in AI servers
Capacitors, including multi-layer ceramic capacitors (MLCCs), function as a “dam” that
temporarily stores electricity, and are an essential electronic component for noise removal

and voltage stabilization in virtually all electronic circuits, including those used in
computers, smartphones, and automobiles. MLCCs play a role in locally stabilizing power
supply (instantaneous current supply plus noise removal) near semiconductors such as
GPUs, CPUs, and FPGAs. Since GPUs, CPUs, and FPGAs repeatedly switch on and off on a
nanosecond timescale, they demand large currents instantaneously, and MLCCs meet this
instantaneous current demand by immediately discharging their stored charge. At the

same time, MLCCs bypass only the AC component (noise and fluctuating current), which
also serves to prevent noise from entering GPUs, CPUs, and FPGAs. MLCCs feature low
ESR (low resistance) and low ESL (low inductance), allowing them to track even

### Page 10

M
Japan Insight
10
nanosecond-level current fluctuations in semiconductors such as GPUs, CPUs, and FPGAs. 
MLCCs are often placed around components such as GPUs and ASICs that operate at low 
voltages of around 1.0V. As semiconductor miniaturization advances, circuit operating 
speed increases and drive voltage falls. As voltage decreases, even small voltage 
fluctuations risk causing semiconductor malfunction, requiring an even more stable power 
supply voltage than before. Demand for capacitor capacitance will also rise along with AI 
servers' improving computing power and increasing power consumption, and because 
board area and mountable space are limited, demand for smaller, higher-capacity MLCCs 
will likely continue. 
MLCC miniaturization and higher capacity to accelerate with 
VR200 NVL72
We assume that the space available for mounting MLCCs on VR200 NVL72 will not 
change significantly from the GB300 NVL72 rack, and that the transition from GB300 to 
VR200 will require installing higher-capacity MLCCs within the limited space of 
semiconductor ABF package substrates and AI accelerator boards, accelerating growth in 
demand for small-sized, high-capacity MLCCs. Murata has consistently been a leader in 
MLCC miniaturization and capacity increases, beginning mass production of 1005-size 1 F 
MLCCs in 2000 and 0402-size 1 F and 1005-size 22 F MLCCs in 2020. We expect 
demand for 1608-size 100 F, 1005-size 47 F, and 0603-size 10 F MLCCs to increase 
substantially with VR200 NVL72. We estimate that 1608-size 100 F MLCCs command a 
unit price 5-10x higher than 2012-size 100 F or 1608-size 47 F MLCCs, and we expect 
the shift to smaller, higher-capacity MLCCs accompanying the transition to VR200 NVL72 
to drive a substantial rise in ASP.

High-capacitance MLCCs (47µF+) are likely to capture a disproportionate share of this 
incremental opportunity: Our analysis suggests that 47µF+ MLCCs could account for 
approximately 50-60% of the incremental Cloud AI MLCC TAM in CY27, representing an 
additional ~US$2B market opportunity alone. This reflects both the increasing use of high-

### Page 11

M
Japan Insight
Morgan Stanley MUFG 11
capacitance devices within AI accelerator systems and a growing mix shift toward higher-
value MLCC content.
From a volume perspective, we estimate Cloud AI demand for 47µF+ MLCCs could 
increase from approximately 4 billion units in CY25 to 40B+ units by CY27: Such a 
rapid increase would represent a meaningful step-up in industry demand and could require 
substantial capacity reallocation or expansion among leading suppliers.
Based on our industry discussions, the market for high-capacitance MLCCs remains 
concentrated among a limited number of suppliers, primarily Japanese and Korean 
manufacturers, including Murata, Taiyo Yuden, and Samsung Electro-Mechanics (SEMCO). 
As demand from AI infrastructure accelerates, we believe capacity utilization for these 
products could tighten, raising the possibility of capacity crowding-out effects across 
other end markets and applications.

Materials development and production technology behind
MLCC miniaturization and capacity increases
In MLCCs, smaller sizes and higher capacities have mainly been achieved by thinning the
dielectric and electrode layers (layer thinning) and increasing the number of layers
stacked (multilayering). For high-capacity products for AI/DC, in addition to the layer
thinning and multilayering described above, efforts are accelerating to further miniaturize

and increase capacity by expanding the area of the dielectric and internal electrodes per
dielectric sheet. We believe MLCC miniaturization and capacity increases depend on: (1)
developing superior dielectric and electrode materials; (2) manufacturing highly uniform,
highly reliable slurry; (3) technology for precisely　stacking dielectric sheets; and (4)
technology for firing in a controlled atmosphere without oxidation. For high-capacity
products for AI/DC, we see the key factors as (1) materials development that increases the

crystallinity of dielectric materials while making them finer, in order to thin each dielectric
layer as much as possible (layer thinning), together with (2) manufacturing technology to
precisely stack the dielectric sheets.
Exhibit 19: MLCC composition
Source: Murata Mfg materials

Source: Morgan Stanley Research
Dielectric material manufacturing process
Dielectric materials are manufactured by adding a binder, solvent, and additives to barium
titanate (BaTiO3), which is produced from barium carbonate (B aCO3) and titanium oxide

(TiO2). Methods for manufacturing barium titanate are broadly divided into the solid-
phase method and the liquid-phase method. In the solid-phase method, barium carbonate
and titanium oxide are mixed and fired at high temperature, producing barium titanate
through a chemical reaction at the firing stage. In the liquid-phase method, barium
carbonate and titanium oxide are dissolved in a solution, reacted at the solution stage, and
fired at a relatively low temperature to produce the compound. Manufacturing methods

used in mass production via the liquid-phase method include the oxalate method and the
hydrothermal synthesis method. Barium titanate with small and uniform particle size is
key to smaller and higher-capacity MLCCs. In 2023, Murata established MF Materials, a
joint venture with Ishihara Sangyo and Ishihara Sangyo's wholly owned subsidiary Fuji
Titanium (with equity stakes of 20% for Murata, 10% for Ishihara Sangyo, and 70% for Fuji
Titanium). MF Materials produces barium titanate via the oxalate process and supplies it

to Murata Manufacturing.　MF Materials is constructing a new building adjacent to its head
office plant in Nobeoka, Miyazaki Prefecture, with operations scheduled to commence in
2027. TDK established a joint venture with Nippon Chemical Industrial in April 2026 called
TDK-NCI Advanced Materials (with equity stakes of 51% for TDK and 49% for Nippon
Chemical Industrial), with plans to accelerate development of the oxalate method barium
titanate.

Murata and SEMCO to benefit the most from growing
demand for high-value-added products for AI servers
We expect Murata and SEMCO, which supply small, high-capacity products stably and in
large volumes, to capture the greatest benefits from growing demand for small-sized,
high-capacity MLCCs used in AI servers and data centers. Murata has explained that AI/DC

MLCC sales accounted for 10-15% of its MLCC sales in F3/26 and are forecast to rise 100-
105% YoY in F3/27, accounting for 20-25% of MLCC sales. We estimate Murata's AI/DC
MLCC sales to rise roughly 120% YoY in F3/27 and about 100% YoY in F3/28, with AI/DC
MLCCs underpinning growth in the company's overall MLCC sales. Murata has stated that
it forecasts a 100-105% increase in AI/DC MLCC sales in F3/27, reflecting a roughly 40%
increase in volume and a roughly 50% rise in ASP, but we expect the increase in ASP to

exceed its forecast.
2025 MLCC market share was Murata 40.8%, Taiyo Yuden
11.3%
We estimate that global MLCC shipment value totaled $14.67bn in 2025 (up 10.3% YoY),
with shares of 40.8% for Murata, 22.5% for SEMCO, 11.3% for Taiyo Yuden, 6.9% for TDK,
and 5.4% for Yageo, and a combined 13.0% for all companies outside these top five. We
expect demand for high-value-added products for AI/DC to keep expanding from 2027,
with Murata and SEMCO continuing to gain market share.

What are the risks to Murata?
Risks for Murata include: (1) losing MLCC market share by failing to adequately supply
MLCCs for AI servers/DCs amid rapidly expanding demand. (2) Murata's products,
including MLCCs, are used not only in AI servers/DCs but across virtually all electronic
equipment, including automobiles, smartphones, and industrial machinery, making the
company sensitive to macroeconomic conditions and inventory cycles. (3) The company

has explained that a ¥1/$ appreciation of the yen would reduce operating profit by ¥4.5bn
per year, and the yen could appreciate beyond our assumed rate of ¥155/$, among other
factors.

Murata Manufacturing earnings forecasts
We expect Murata at 2Q results (end-Oct) to hike F3/27
guidance; continued earnings growth from F3/28
F3/27-28 and F3/27 2Q forecasts: We project F3/27 OP of ¥490.8bn (guidance ¥430bn,
FactSet consensus ¥453.9bn), up ¥208.9bn YoY and above F3/22's record of ¥424.1bn.
That is because we expect profit in the Components segment to rise ¥186.2bn to ¥501.3bn
and losses in the Device & Modules segment to shrink by ¥24.1bn from -¥26.5bn to -

¥2.4bn. We look for Components profit to rise due to a +31.6% YoY increase in MLCC
sales in dollar terms (+36.1% on yen basis), a continued increase after F3/26’s +13.8% rise
(+12.6% on yen basis), product mix improvement, and higher utilization rates absorbing
increased depreciation and other fixed cost growth. We expect Device & Modules losses
to shrink mainly due to the falling out of ¥43.8bn in Resonant goodwill impairment and
¥6bn from the disposal of the micro battery business. We project F3/28 OP of ¥702.6bn

(consensus ¥655.3bn). The projected ¥211.8bn YoY growth breaks down as Components +
¥170bn and Devices & Modules +¥39.6bn. We expect Components profit to rise thanks to
a 24.5% YoY increase in capacitor (MLCC) sales in dollars (+23.7% on yen basis) and
continued product mix improvement absorbing increased depreciation & other fixed cost
growth. We look for Devices & Modules profit to rise on factors including increased sales
of high-frequency modules, secondary batteries, and vertical power delivery modules.

We project 2Q OP of ¥125bn (consensus ¥122.9bn). The projected QoQ ¥26.6bn growth
breaks down as Components +¥12.5bn QoQ to ¥126bn, and Devices & Modules +¥13.9bn
QoQ to ¥1bn. For Components, we expect sales of MLCCs for AI/data centers to continue
to increase, the MLCC product mix to improve, and utilization rates to continue to rise. For
Devices & Modules, we expect sales for North American smartphones to increase
seasonally and reenter the black, but because North American smartphone production is

split between fall models and spring models, profit contributions are likely to be limited.

Murata’s share price risk/reward: Even though we raise our earnings estimates for

Murata, we lower our DCF-based price target from ¥12,500 to ¥11,000, mainly reflecting
an increase in our DCF model’s risk-free rate from 2.60% to 3.0%. Murata's share price has
fallen 41% year-to-date from its June 22 peak, and we see 53% upside over the next 12-18
months, maintain our OW rating, and designate the stock as our Top Pick in Japan's
electronic components industry. The reasons are as follows: (1) We forecast the dollar-
based shipment value of MLCCs, where Murata held the top global share of 40% in 2025,

to expand at a CAGR of 21% through 2031, with Murata continuing to gradually gain
market share. (2) Demand for MLCCs used in AI servers/DCs is likely to continue
expanding, and demand for small, high-capacity, high-value-added products, which Murata
has brought to market ahead of competitors, should grow substantially. (3) Outside of
MLCCs, we look for demand to expand for small, high-value-added products, including
secondary batteries for DC BBUs, power modules for vertical power delivery in AI servers,

and RF modules for smartphones, and then expand even further as edge AI devices

### Page 18

M
Japan Insight
18
become widespread. (4) In addition to earnings growth, we expect that management’s
proactive approach to shareholder returns, including share buybacks, will raise capital
efficiency and lift ROE.
Bull case ¥14,000, 95% projected upside
In our bull case, we assume higher sales of MLCCs for AI servers/data centers than in our
base case, for component sales of 7% higher than our base case, for OP of ¥560.3bn in
F3/27, ¥794bn in F3/28, and ¥1.515tn in F3/31. Our forex assumptions and DCF

assumptions are the same as for our base case. Bull case fair value corresponds to 58.2x of
fully-diluted F3/27 EPS of ¥240.4, 41.1x fully-diluted F3/28 EPS of ¥340.7, and 21.1x fully-
diluted F3/31 EPS of ¥664.0.
Bear case ¥3,500
Murata products are used in all kinds of different electronic devices, and they are easily
affected by the macro economy and inventory cycle. In our bear case, we assume slower
global economic growth rates and lower production of high-end consumer goods like

high-end smartphones than in our base case for OP of ¥314.6bn in F3/27, ¥330bn in F3/28,
and ¥362.4bn in F3/31. Our forex assumptions and DCF assumptions are the same as in
our base case. Here we look for ROE to increase from 8.8% in F3/26 to 9.3% in F3/27 and
9.4% in F3/28, above capital cost of 7.5%. With profit below the market consensus, we
assume the share price falls to a P/B of 2.4x.

SEMCO earnings forecasts
Shawn Kim
AI-driven MLCC growth and pricing to drive sharp earnings
acceleration in FY27, with continued growth in FY28
FY26-28 forecasts: We project SEMCO OP of W2.34tn in FY26 (+156% YoY), W5.70tn in
FY27 and W8.36tn in FY28, implying further growth of 144% and 47%, respectively. We
expect the earnings acceleration to be driven mainly by the Components (MLCC) and
Package Solution businesses, while the Optics business remains a relatively modest

contributor. In FY27, our projected OP of W5.70tn breaks down as Components W4.56tn,
Package Solution W943bn, and Optics W201bn, versus W1.67tn, W510bn, and W165bn,
respectively, in FY26. In FY28, we forecast Components OP rising further to W6.55tn,
Package Solution to W1.58tn, and Optics to W230bn. As such, we expect SEMCO's consol.
OP margin to grow from 8.1% in FY25 to 15.7% in FY26, 24.5% in FY27, and 25.9% in FY28.
We maintain the stock as our top pick within our S. Korea Technology coverage.

For Components, we expect profit growth to accelerate as AI-server demand drives both
strong MLCC volume growth and a structurally richer product mix. High-end AI MLCC
supply remains tight, with lead times exceeding 20 weeks, supply-chain inventories lean
following two years of destocking, and book-to-bill at the three high-end suppliers
reaching its highest level since the pandemic. Importantly, contract pricing has increasingly
been institutionalized through long-term agreements, while the high-end AI MLCC market

remains concentrated, with Murata and SEMCO together holding around 85% share.
Capacity additions are also unlikely to close the supply-demand gap quickly, as most
meaningful new capacity is scheduled for 2027 or later and suppliers are generally
targeting only around 10-15% annual output growth. We thus expect tighter supply-
demand, higher pricing, and a shift toward high-capacitance, low-ESL AI products to drive
Components OP margin from 21.4% in FY26 to 30.9% in FY27 and 30.1% in FY28.

Bull case W3,000,000
Our bull case assumes ABF opportunities from major US CSPs drive stronger content
growth, with future ASIC content & volume growth continuing to outpace our base-case
expectations. Customer diversification toward non-mobile customers should also reduce
earnings volatility and improve profitability. Our bull case, equivalent to 15x FY27e P/B,
reflects what we see as an unprecedented super-cycle in the underlying AI-related MLCC

and ABF markets.
Bear case W1,350,000
In our bear case, we assume slower content growth in MLCCs and substrates due to
greater competition and China's localization, together with prolonged consumer weakness
weighing on the Optics business. However, we still expect increasing AI exposure to
structurally lift SEMCO's MLCC and ABF ASPs, product mix and margins, given the
difficulty commodity suppliers face in penetrating the premium market. Our bear-case

value corresponds to 7x FY27e P/B. Key downside risks include a significant pullback in

### Page 22

M
Japan Insight
22
Samsung's mobile flagship cycle, lower SEMCO market share, execution risk in penetrating
Chinese smartphone customers, and continued consumer-demand weakness.

Morgan Stanley MUFG 25
Yageo earnings forecasts
Howard Kao
Tightening MLCC Narrative is Unchanged
We forecast CY26 revenue of approximately NT$183B, up 38% y/y, driven primarily by
stronger AI-related demand, with a more modest contribution from consumer
applications. This demand strength should support broad-based utilization improvement
across Yageo’s passive component portfolio. Pricing is also turning more favorable, with
distributor price increases implemented in 2Q26 and Yageo raising prices for direct

customers in 3Q26, as previously highlighted. The combination of higher utilization and
firmer pricing should drive meaningful operating leverage. We forecast gross margin
expanding to 39.3%, up 310bps y/y, and operating margin reaching 27.4%, up 500bps y/y,
supporting EPS of approximately NT$19.79.
Looking into CY27, we expect supply/demand conditions in the mid- and low-end markets
to tighten further, sustaining pricing momentum and driving an additional improvement in

utilization. We therefore forecast revenue of approximately NT$252B, up 38% y/y.
Continued operating leverage should lift gross margin to 43.2%, an increase of 390bps y/y,
and operating margin to 33.4%, up 600bps y/y, resulting in EPS of approximately NT$32.11.
Why has the stock been so volatile?
Yageo benefited from a sharp increase in MLCC pricing in 2Q26, driven by a temporary
spike in spot-market demand. In our view, ODM/EMS customers accelerated inventory

replenishment amid concerns that the ramp in high-end AI-server MLCC demand,
particularly alongside Nvidia’s Vera Rubin platform, could constrain supply of mid- and
low-end MLCCs. Under normal market conditions, these customers typically carry only 3-4
weeks of inventory. However, supply concerns prompted a rapid increase in inventory
coverage to approximately 4-6 months.
During this restocking phase, ODM/EMS customers sourced MLCCs from both
manufacturers, including Yageo, and distributors. The resulting demand surge drove a

sharp increase in spot/distributor pricing and also contributed to the stock’s strong
performance. However, the pricing move was largely driven by a one-off inventory build.
Once customers had secured sufficient inventory, purchasing activity normalized and spot
pricing began to soften. This, together with weaker broader market sentiment, led to a
pullback of ~50% from the stock’s peak of approximately NT$1,140.
At the current share price, however, we believe the risk-reward has become attractive. The

underlying fundamentals, and, importantly, our view of the MLCC upcycle, remain intact.
Our analysis indicates substantial incremental demand for high-end, high-capacitance
MLCCs of 47µF and above, which will require a meaningful increase in dedicated
production capacity. We expect this to drive further capacity conversion toward high-end
MLCCs, with the resulting crowding-out effect becoming more visible from late CY26 into
early CY27. This should progressively tighten supply in the mid- and low-end MLCC

market and provide renewed support for pricing.

Price Target Discussion and Valuation Methodology
We lower our 12-month price target to NT$1,050 (from NT$1,465): This reflects our
lower earnings estimates for CY26-28. Our price target is our base case value, derived
from our residual income valuation model. Similar to our analysis of other tech hardware
companies within our coverage, we think this methodology provides the most accurate

value of the firm by taking into account the cost of equity.
Our key assumptions are:
• Cost of equity of 7.8%:
° Risk-free rate of 1.0% (10-year Taiwanese government bond yield);
° Equity risk premium of 6.8%;
° Beta of 1.1;

### Page 27

M
Japan Insight
Morgan Stanley MUFG 27
• Medium-term growth rate of 12% (down from 16%);
• Terminal growth rate of 3%.
Our price target implies 33x 2027e P/E and 24x 2028e P/E. These are above its five-year
historical range of 8x to 18x, which we view as justified considering its longer-term
transformation story to become a global total solutions provider, moving from passive
into active components. In addition, compared to global passive component peers, we

believe Yageo stock remains undervalued with higher ROE and margins but lower P/E vs.
Japanese peers (which trade on an average at 40x+ P/E).
Our bull and bear case scenario values decrease by magnitudes similar to the change
to our base case value, to NT$1,490 and NT$480, respectively.

Taiyo Yuden earnings forecasts
We expect expect record profit in F3/29, not F3/28; upgrade
the stock from UW to EW
Full-term F3/27 and F3/28 earnings forecasts, F3/27 2Q forecasts: We project F3/27 OP
of ¥45.1bn (guidance ¥45bn, FactSet consensus ¥42bn), up +¥25.1bn YoY. We expect sales
in dollars of core product capacitors (MLCCs) to increase +22.6% YoY (+27.4% on yen
basis), a continued increase following on FY3/26’s +10.4% rise (+8.5% on yen basis), and

higher utilization rates absorbing increased depreciation expense and other fixed cost
increases. We project F3/28 OP of ¥66.1bn (consensus ¥73.1bn), up +¥25bn YoY. We look
for sales in dollars of capacitors (MLCCs) to rise +17.3% YoY (+16.6% on a yen basis),
absorbing increased depreciation expense and other fixed cost increases. We expect OP to
rise ¥83.8bn in F3/29 (consensus ¥107.1bn), surpassing the historical high profit of
¥68.2bn in F3/22.

We project 2Q OP of ¥12.6bn (consensus ¥11bn), up +¥7.8bn from ¥4.8bn in 1Q. We look
for MLCC sales to rise +18.1% compared with 1Q and for the utilization rate to rise from
85% in 1Q to 95% in 2Q. With increased sales of inductors for North American
smartphones, there should be a major profit increase on 1Q.

Note: Consensus is based on FactSet; Source: Fact Set, Morgan Stanley Research
Taiyo Yuden share price risk/reward: We upgrade Taiyo Yuden from UW to EW, as we
believe its share price, having fallen 56.0% from its July 1 peak, has limited downside risk

and offers 6% upside over the next 12-18 months. We lower our DCF-based price target
from ¥12,500 to ¥10,500. This mainly reflects an increase in the DCF model’s risk-free
rate from 2.60% to 3.0%. We rate the stock EW rather than OW because: (1) In its core
MLCC business, we estimate Taiyo Yuden's MLCC market share was 11.3% in 2025, down
from 12.7% in 2020, and we think this downtrend continued into 2026, with its market
share shrinking to 10.7% in 1Q and 9.9% in 2Q. (2) We believe Taiyo Yuden is lagging

behind Murata (No. 1 share) and SEMCO (No. 2) in small, high-capacity MLCCs for AI
server accelerator boards, where demand is rapidly expanding. (3) We expect consensus
estimates for F3/28 onward to be revised down after 2Q results.
Bull case ¥14,000, 49% projected upside
In our bull case, F3/27 sales of MLCCs for AI servers/data centers are 7% higher than in our
base case, for OP of ¥56.5bn in F3/27, ¥85.5bn in F3/28, and ¥176.3bn in F3/31. Our forex

assumptions and DCF assumptions match our base case. Bull case fair value corresponds
to 50.7x our fully-diluted F3/27 EPS estimate of ¥276.1, 33.6x our fully-diluted F3/28 EPS
estimate of ¥417.0, and 15.9x our full-diluted F3/31 EPS estimate of ¥879.1.
Bear case ¥4,500
Taiyo Yuden’s care products (MLCCs and inductors) are used in all kinds of electronic
devices, and are easily affected by the macro economy and inventory cycle. In our bear
case, we assume slower global economic growth rates and lower production of high-end

consumer goods like high-end smartphones than in our base case, for OP of ¥39.2bn in
F3/27, ¥56.1bn in F3/28, and ¥65.8bn in F3/31. Our forex assumptions and DCF
assumptions are the same as in our base case. Here we look for ROE of 7.3% in F3/27 and
10.1% in F3/28, improving to a level above capital cost of 7.9%. With profit below market
expectations, we assume the share price falls to a P/B of 1.8x.

CHIEF INVESTMENT OFFICE
Capital Market Outlook

### Page 2

2 of 8 September 8, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
Commodities: From Inflation Hedge to Structural Growth Asset
Anna Snider, Managing Director and Head of Investment Manager Selection
(Due Diligence)
A broader role in portfolios: For decades, commodities occupied a relatively narrow
role in portfolios: protection against inflation when rising prices eroded the value
of traditional assets. That function remains important, but the investment case is

broadening. Commodities now sit at the intersection of geopolitical fragmentation,
supply-chain security, reshoring, inventory rebuilding, and the buildout of AI and digital
infrastructure. Together, these forces are turning parts of the asset class from purely
cyclical exposures into potential long-term growth beneficiaries.
The diversification case is also becoming more relevant. In Q1, when inflation concerns
peaked, the Bloomberg Commodity Index returned close to 25%, outperforming both

Equities and Fixed Income. At the same time, the relationship among major asset
classes has shifted. Equity and Fixed Income correlations have tightened in recent years,
while commodities have moved in the opposite direction: over the trailing five years,
their correlation with both Equities and bonds has been negative. That combination of
inflation sensitivity and differentiated return behavior can make commodities useful
beyond a simple tactical hedge.

AI infrastructure is a physical buildout: AI is often viewed through semiconductors,
software platforms, and hyperscale data centers. Yet the infrastructure required to
deploy AI is extraordinarily commodity intensive. It requires continuous electricity,
generation capacity, transmission lines, substations, transformers, cooling systems,
networking hardware and structural materials. In that sense, AI is not only a computing
revolution. It is also an energy, infrastructure, and critical-materials investment cycle.

Power is the first constraint. Data centers require reliable, around-the-clock electricity.
Natural gas remains a practical source of incremental generation in the U.S., while
nuclear power is emerging as a strategic long-term option for hyperscalers seeking
stable baseload supply. Solar and wind can add capacity and support decarbonization
goals, but grid reliability and storage become more important as power demand rises.
That supports investment in batteries, backup power and grid-scale storage, with lithium

and graphite among the relevant inputs.
The buildout also increases demand for industrial metals. Copper is a clear beneficiary
because it is essential to transmission, transformers, cooling systems and data-center
wiring. Aluminum and iron ore are used in facilities, electrical equipment, transmission
networks and supporting infrastructure. Semiconductor fabrication adds another layer
of demand through industrial gases such as helium and neon, high-purity quartz for

wafers and optics, and gallium and germanium for advanced chips, photonics, and
telecommunications. AI-enabled automation and robotics also depend on permanent
magnets that use rare earth elements, including neodymium-praseodymium and
dysprosium-terbium.
Demand has moved faster than supply: This demand surge resembles earlier cycles
in semiconductors and memory, where expectations pulled spending forward. Metals
and mining companies are already seeing earnings growth and margin expansion,

yet the industry has struggled to keep pace with demand. The imbalance matters
because commodity supply is slow to respond. Production of many strategic materials
is geographically concentrated, permitting timelines are lengthy, recycling capacity is
limited, and substitution can remain years from commercial viability. New mines and
processing facilities can take a decade or more to reach production.
Investment Implications
Commodities may increasingly serve

three portfolio purposes: inflation
protection, diversification from
traditional assets, and participation in
long-term infrastructure and resource-
demand growth, supporting a diversified
allocation across direct commodities
and select producer Equities rather than
a single directional trade.

### Page 3

3 of 8 September 8, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
Governments are also taking a larger role through industrial policy, export controls,
stockpiling and efforts to secure critical supply chains. Restrictions involving gallium,
germanium, graphite and other strategic materials underscore how geopolitics can affect
availability and pricing. As a result, the current cycle may produce greater scarcity and
pricing power than demand growth alone would suggest.

So far, this dynamic has been most visible in industrial metals, but the same forces could
extend into agriculture. Reshoring changes where food and inputs are produced and
processed, while inventory rebuilding can lift demand for fertilizer, fuel, machinery and
storage. Agriculture has not yet experienced the same earnings and pricing impact as
metals, but supply-chain resilience could make it a broader beneficiary over time.
Cyclical forces still matter: The structural thesis does not eliminate near-term

volatility. Energy remains a key transmission mechanism for inflation because oil and
natural gas underpin transportation, manufacturing and industrial production. Geopolitical
disruptions can cascade through supply chains, raising costs and moving both commodity
futures and spot prices. Currency dynamics matter as well. A stronger U.S. dollar can
restrain commodity prices by increasing costs for non-U.S. buyers, while renewed dollar
weakness could support emerging-market demand and reprice dollar-denominated

assets.
A stabilization of geopolitical tensions could create a more constructive cyclical backdrop
through lower energy volatility, improved business confidence and stronger capital
investment. Conversely, renewed conflict can produce sharp and uneven moves across
the complex. These crosscurrents reinforce the value of treating commodities as a
diversified allocation rather than a single directional trade.
Gold, implementation and the portfolio case: Gold is fundamentally different from

most industrial commodities, but it deserves attention. Its traditional relationships with
interest rates and the dollar have been less reliable lately. Even so, sustained central bank
and exchange-traded fund buying points to durable long-term demand despite bouts of
volatility such as the selloff earlier this year. Gold also tended to move inversely with oil
during the height of the Iran conflict, illustrating how different commodities can respond

differently to the same geopolitical shock.
Investors can access the theme through commodity futures or producer Equities. Direct
commodity exposure is generally the clearer vehicle for inflation protection, scarcity, and
geopolitical supply shocks. Producer equities can offer greater upside when commodity
prices rise faster than operating costs because earnings and margins expand, but that
leverage is asymmetric. Company-specific execution, financing, cost and valuation risks

can still cause Equities to underperform the underlying commodity. A blended approach
may capture both physical scarcity and corporate operating leverage.
Ultimately, commodities remain sensitive to energy markets, currencies, growth, and
geopolitics, but their portfolio role is expanding. They can provide inflation protection and
diversification while offering exposure to AI infrastructure, electrification, energy security,
defense modernization, reshoring and, potentially, agricultural supply-chain investment.

The strongest beneficiaries of the AI trade may therefore extend well beyond technology
companies to the materials and power systems that make deployment possible.

### Page 4

4 of 8 September 8, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MARKET VIEW
Rising Global Interest Rates and International Markets
Ehiwario Efeyini, Director and Senior Investment Strategist
Strong earnings growth has so far delivered a fourth consecutive year of double-digit
gains for global Equity indexes in 2026, propelling them to new all-time highs. But a
growing risk throughout the year has been the increase in government bond yields, which

have also broken out to multi-decade highs across a number of the major markets. The
U.S. Treasury will this week begin its expanded Treasury securities buyback program
announced in mid-August in response to rising rates. But 10-year yields across all
the major G10 countries have moved higher, in many cases by as much as or more
than in the U.S. market. Against a backdrop of above-trend growth in nominal gross
domestic product (GDP), high energy prices and elevated inflation, investors appear to

be reassessing monetary policy expectations, particularly in Europe where most of the
2026 increase in 10-year yields has been driven by inflation breakevens (Exhibit 1). Fixed
Income markets are consequently looking for policy rate hikes from the European Central
Bank later this week and potentially from the Bank of Japan later next month.
The rise in global interest rates has also come alongside an expansion in fiscal policy
across the major markets. In Europe, 2026 and 2027 are expected to be the peak

spending years for defense and infrastructure expenditure plans unveiled last year. The
so-called Readiness 2030 package allocates €800 billion to increased European Union
defense spending as it looks to move toward its 5% of GDP target by 2035. And at the
same time, the German government relaxation of its national debt brake has directed
€500 billion toward funding for investment in clean energy, transportation and digital
infrastructure. Similarly in Japan, the government supermajority is looking to enact a

new stimulus aimed at raising investment levels across 17 strategic sectors such as
semiconductors, defense and shipbuilding, in addition to a planned two-year cut to the
consumption tax on food from April 2027. And in the UK, the new central government
leadership is also likely to announce new spending measures when its first national
budget is released next month. To the extent that increased public investment spending
can lift productivity and support nominal GDP growth in Europe and Japan, the rise in

yields need not undermine economic growth or derail the equity market advance. But
higher rates, especially in response to supply-driven energy price inflation, nonetheless
remain a risk for developed market equity returns going into the end of the year.
Exhibit 1: Global Bond Yields in Major Markets have Risen by as Much or More
than U.S. Yields in 2026.

For many emerging markets the picture looks quite different. In contrast with the U.S.,
Japan and Europe, inflation rates are generally well below central bank targets outside
a limited number of resource exporters in Latin America and the booming AI-linked
economies of Korea and Taiwan (Exhibit 2). As a result, policy rate expectations over

the coming quarters are stable or lower for several emerging markets (including China,
Malaysia, Thailand, Poland, Brazil and Mexico) according to Bloomberg consensus forecasts.

and corporate earnings should support
global equity markets even in the face
of rising rates. For Emerging Markets

inflation, flat to lower local policy rate
expectations and stronger fundamentals

continue to favor EM Equities over

Historically, rising U.S. interest rates have been a major headwind for emerging markets.
But looking ahead in the current cycle, we still expect the impact of potential Federal
Reserve (Fed) rate hikes to be lower than in the past. Over recent years, the association
between tighter Fed policy and weaker EM returns appears to have diminished. EM
Equities for example were resilient in the face of aggressive U.S. rate increases in 2022
and 2023, broadly keeping pace with the rest of the world. This is in sharp contrast to

prior cycles in which tighter Fed policy has resulted in capital flight, deleveraging and
falling asset values across the emerging world. Particularly during the crisis periods of
the 1980s and 1990s—but also more recently during the 2015 taper tantrum—U.S.
monetary tightening has tended to attract capital away from riskier emerging countries
(particularly those with large current account deficits), reducing support for local asset
prices and exchange rates.

Associated strength in the U.S. dollar has typically been an additional hurdle. For those
emerging economies with fixed exchange rates, a loss of export competitiveness has
further undermined current account positions, forcing central banks to run down their
foreign exchange reserves. And where exchange rates are floating, foreign currency
debts have risen in local currency terms, resulting in widespread defaults, debt
downgrades, recessions and additional exchange rate depreciation.

But starting in the years that followed the 1997-1998 Asian financial crisis and
continuing into the current cycle, the fundamentals across much of the EM universe have
improved. Fixed exchange rates have been abandoned. EM foreign currency reserves have
roughly doubled as a share of GDP . The share of EM debt denominated in U.S. dollars
has fallen significantly, led by the private sector across households and corporates. And
for most countries, current account deficits have narrowed or disappeared entirely. As a

result, emerging markets have become much more insulated from the direction of U.S.
interest rates on a structural basis, and this has only been reinforced by the AI-driven
revaluation of key Asian markets in 2026. Greater concentration in Korea and Taiwan has
increased the EM weighting in the surplus Asia-Pacific region to 83% as at the end of
August—up from 81% at the start of the year and 71% a decade ago.
Above-trend growth in nominal GDP and ongoing strength in corporate earnings should

support global equity markets even in the face of rising interest rates. But for emerging
Equities in particular, contained inflation relative to central bank targets, flat to lower
local policy rate expectations and stronger economic fundamentals should limit the
impact from higher global rates. We continue to favor EM Equities over International
Developed Markets, where inflation levels are generally still above target, central banks
are expected to raise interest rates and investors will be watching for fiscal sustainability

as governments implement expansionary budget plans.

### Page 6

6 of 8 September 8, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THOUGHT OF THE WEEK
The One-Offs Inflating Q2 Earnings
Lauren Sanfilippo, Director and Senior Investment Strategist
Headline Q2 earnings numbers look almost too good to be true. And in a sense, they are.
With nearly all of S&P 500 companies’ results reported, EPS growth is tracking around
50% year-over-year (YoY)—not just an acceleration from Q1 but a second consecutive
quarter above 20%. Chiefly skewing the headline number are massive mark-to-market

gains on private investments for a handful of mega cap technology companies like
Alphabet and Amazon that booked outsized non-operating gains tied to their stakes in
Anthropic, while Microsoft benefited from its OpenAI investment. Strip those out, and
adjusted EPS growth notches down to 31%. The point being, after being sliced and
diced, 30%+ is well above any long-run historical earnings growth rate.
The one-off nature of earnings strength wasn’t limited to investment gains. A second,

less-discussed boost was tariff refunds. As Exhibit 3 illustrates, revenues from tariffs
steadily climbed from early 2025 through mid-2026 until the International Emergency
Economic Powers Act (IEEPA) tariff ruling which initiated the refund process. As
refunds were disbursed, customs receipts turned net negative in June and July, with
refund payments exceeding total tariff collections. Customs and Border Protection cash
withdrawals of more than $100 billion in refunds have now been paid—60% of the $165

billion in total eligible claims.
The recipients: the “importers of record,” or the dozens of S&P 500 companies that
have disclosed billions in refunds over the past two quarters; Apple alone received $2.19
billion, adding $0.11 to per-share earnings to their quarter. Other disclosed refunds
include Nike with $986 million, Amazon with $640 million, and General Motors refunded
$500 million (although accounting treatment and use of proceeds has varied).

The boost of investment gains and/or refunds may not be repeatable in future quarters.
Perhaps more telling is that the median company’s revenue grew roughly 15% YoY, the
strongest gain in years, corroborating that there is operating momentum rather than
just an accounting mirage for Q2 earnings. Margins, too, as a percent of GDP are at
their highest back to 1950, hovering north of 22%, see Exhibit 4. That this is occurring
amid rising rates and at a time of geopolitical uncertainty/disruption, rather than a post-

recessionary recovery, makes the results that much more impressive.
The Path Forward: Questions remain about the sustainability of these elevated growth
rates, although consensus expects EPS growth to stay above 20% in both Q3 and Q4.
If achieved, every quarter this year would see 20%-plus EPS growth. On the back of
brighter outlooks from Corporate America in recent weeks, full-year earnings growth
will decelerate into the mid-teens in 2027, but that moderation comes off a high base

of 2026. For now, the breadth of earnings growth is the encouraging story as 10 of
the 11 sectors reported growth (Energy and Communication Services leading the way,
Healthcare the only detractor). That’s a reflection of strong core businesses growing
revenues into 2027’s tougher YoY comparisons.
Investment Implications
Underlying earnings-per-share (EPS)
growth of around 30%, stripped
of investment gains, is historically
exceptional. It remains that robust

corporate earnings and positive
revisions have helped Equities absorb
concerns around the recent rise
in yields.

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.

### Page 1

#gdp #newzealand
First impressions: NZ GDP, June quarter 2026
By Michael Gordon
Senior Economist, Westpac NZ
09:01 September 17 2026 Share
The New Zealand economy grew by 0.2% in the June quarter, in line with our forecast,
although it was a little softer in the details than we expected.
Key results
Quarterly change: +0.2% (last: +0.9%, revised from +0.8%)
Westpac f/c: +0.2%, market f/c: +0.1%, RBNZ: 0.0%

### Page 2

Annual change: +2.6% (last: +1.7%, revised from +1.5%)
Westpac f/c: +2.4%
The June quarter national accounts reinforce our message that the New Zealand economy has largely
held its ground through the US-Iran conflict, though it hasn’t been completely unscathed. A few sectors
in particular have taken the brunt of the surge in fuel prices since March, but the greater impact is
probably more a matter of what might have been: the momentum that was building in late 2025 and

early 2026 might have progressed further were it not for this latest headwind.
GDP rose by 0.2% for the quarter, in line with our forecast. This was slightly ahead of the median
market forecast of 0.1% and the RBNZ’s forecast of a flat outturn in their September Monetary Policy
Statement (which didn’t have the benefit of the final data releases that led us and the market to
upgrade our forecasts).
As expected, the largest growth contribution came from construction, up 2.7%. There were also solid

contributions from manufacturing, wholesaling, and information and telecommunications. The main
declines were in transport and hospitality, which likely reflects the impact of the surge in fuel prices –
households have tightened their belts by cutting back on travel in particular.
It was a ‘high’ read for the quarter – 0.247% unrounded – and March quarter growth was revised up
from 0.8% to 0.9%, largely due to updated data on construction activity. Together these meant that

annual growth accelerated to 2.6%, ahead of our 2.4% estimate.
However, the results for the June quarter were a little weaker in the details than we were expecting.
Firstly, the non-additive balancing item (as we calculate it, which is a little different from what Stats NZ
reports) knocked around 0.2ppts off growth for the quarter, less than the 0.3ppts that we had assumed.
Furthermore, we were surprised to the downside on balance for the ‘market’ sectors of the economy,

with softer than expected results in agriculture, transport and administrative services. The latter two will
likely reflect the impact of higher fuel prices on travel and tourism (admin services includes tour
operators and travel agencies). In contrast, there were surprisingly large gains in healthcare (up 0.8%)
and central government administration (up 2.1%, though this came after a 0.8% fall in the March
quarter).
For the RBNZ, these results will help to assuage some of the committee members’ concerns about

downside risks to growth. But our view remains that the committee will have more of an eye to the
evidence on inflation pressures, such as the NZIER business survey on the 6  of October and the CPI
on the 22 , as well as the rapidly shifting picture for oil prices, to determine their next move.
Michael Gordon, Senior Economist

### Page 2

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
The six-month annualised growth rate in the Westpac–
Melbourne Institute Leading Index, which indicates the likely
pace of economic activity relative to trend three to nine months
into the future, lifted to –0.09% in August from –0.17% in July.

The Leading Index shows momentum has continued to
improve and is only a touch below trend heading into late
2026 and early 2027. The June quarter national accounts
showed the Australian economy holding up better than
expected in the face of a global energy shock and higher
interest rates. Growth appears to be stabilising at a slow
pace rather than stalling with households resilient and
notable support coming from a strong ramp-up in data centre
investment. While a further rise in interest rates will take

some of the gloss off near term, Westpac now expects annual
growth to slow 1.5% by year-end, upgraded from our previous
forecast of 1% and broadly consistent with the improved
signal from the Leading Index.
The growth pulse is still clearly on the soft side. The Leading
Index growth rate has been below trend since the start of the
year, averaging –0.17%. This compares to the slightly above
trend reads recorded through most of 2025, which posted an

average read of +0.26%. However, the current below trend
run is noticeably milder than the weakness seen during the
protracted cost-of-living crisis that ran through most of 2022
to 2024. The average Leading Index growth read during this
period was –0.46% with lows closer to –1%.
The Leading Index growth rate is now roughly back where it
was six months ago, down only slightly from the –0.07% pace
back in February. That said, the detail points to significant

shifts in the growth mix and some fragility to the improved
signal. Component-wise the six months has seen slightly
bigger drags coming from labour markets, financial markets,
commodity prices and consumer sentiment, which have
taken 0.42ppts off the Index growth rate since February on
a combined basis. This has been balanced by a more positive
signal from dwelling approvals (adding +0.32ppts) and a
firming in US industrial production (+0.08ppts).

The mix does raise some questions about the extent to which
the recent improvement in momentum will be sustained.
Leading Index suggests momentum a touch below trend
The Westpac-Melbourne Institute Leading Index of
Economic Activity is designed to assist in identifying
turning points in the economy. The index combines
variables that reflect different aspects of the economy
into a single index that generally produces a more reliable
cyclical indicator than any single component taken

individually. The Leading Index of Economic Activity
provides advance information on the state of the economy
and gives early warnings of cyclical turning points.
Rising fuel prices and concerns about further interest rate
increases look to be weighing on consumer sentiment again
with signs that the downturn in established housing markets
is also impacting. These effects could intensify near term and
may spread beyond the consumer to other index components.

The Reserve Bank Monetary Policy Board next meets on
September 28–29. While the latest Leading Index update
still shows the growth momentum is below trend, the soft
rather than weak pace suggests the economy’s relatively
resilient performance in the June quarter is likely to extend
through the second half of the year and early part of 2027.
This performance is adding to the Board’s unease that high
inflation remains ‘sticky’ and may track a slower path back to

the 2-3% target. We expect the RBA to raise rates again but,
on balance, hold off until another comprehensive quarterly
inflation update is released on October 28. That means the
September meeting is likely to be a ‘very hawkish hold’.
Matthew Hassan
Head of Australian Macro-Forecasting

名目潜在成長率
（投資リターン）

実物投資の利ざや

米長期金利５％超は警戒水準 
米国の10 年国債利回りが５％を超えた。これは単なる心理的な
節目ではなく、実体経済・株式市場の双方にとって警戒すべき水
準だ。図表１でマクロ環境を確認すると、長期金利は米国経済
の巡航速度とされる名目潜在成長率を上回っている。投資の期
待リターンよりも資金調達コストの方が高くなれば、実物資産投
資は抑制されやすい。住宅市場など、一部で金融環境の引き締
まりが鮮明化しており、これが景気悪化につながるリスクが高ま
っている。

図表１：米国の長期金利は名目潜在成長率を超過

注：シャドーは景気後退局面、潜在成長率は議会予算局推計  
出所：米議会予算局、Bloomberg、ソニーFG

株式市場への影響も無視できない。図表２の通り、長期金利は
Ｓ＆Ｐ５００指数の株式益利回りに肉薄している。無リスクの国債
で株式並みの利回りを確保できる状況では、投資家がリスクを
取る合理性が薄れるため、株価にはバリュエーション調整圧力
が掛かりやすい。今後さらに長期金利が上昇すれば、実物投資
から貯蓄へ、株式市場から債券市場へと資金シフトが進み、景
気減速と株安を招き得る。

図表２：イールドスプレッドの消滅で、株式に割高感

注：株式益利回り＝一株当たり純利益／株価  
出所：Bloomberg、ソニーFG

長期金利＝期待政策金利＋ﾀｰﾑﾌﾟﾚﾐｱﾑ 
図表３は米長期金利を、ＦＲＢの金融政策見通しを表す「期待政
策金利」と、財政リスクや国債需給を反映する「タームプレミアム」
に分解したものである。

図表３：長期金利＝期待政策金利＋タームプレミアム

注：期待政策金利とタームプレミアムはNY連銀のACMモデルの推計値  
出所：NY連銀、Bloomberg、ソニーFG 
 米長期金利が５％を突破。長期金利は名目潜在成長率を上回り、住宅市場などの一部で金融環境の引き
締まりがみられる。株式益利回りにも肉薄しており、景気減速と株安を警戒すべき水準  
 米金利上昇の背景はインフレと財政懸念の高まり。当面の米 10 年金利は 4.5～5.5％での推移を見込む  
 中間選挙前のポピュリズム的な財政政策は、インフレ・財政不安・金利上昇の悪循環を招く  
 市場の吸収力にも懸念。ＦＲＢの資産圧縮、海外中銀の米債売却、ＡＩ企業との資金争奪戦、インフレ懸念  
 米 30 年金利は財政リスクによる上振れで、当面は 4.9～5.9％での推移を予想。米金利の上昇は日本の超
長期金利にも波及する公算 
KEY 
POINT

24/01 24/07 25/01 25/07 26/01 26/07
（経済政策不確実性指数）
（年/月）
タームプレミアム
（右軸）
経済政策不確実性指数
（左軸）
（タームプレミアム、％）
イラン戦争以降の金利上昇は、原油高を背景としたインフレ懸
念と利上げ観測の高まりが主因であることがわかる。ただし、ト
ランプ政権発足後に高まったタームプレミアムも高水準を維持し
ており、現在の高金利は金融政策要因だけでは説明できない。

タームプレミアム： 
関心はトランプリスクから財政・需給懸念へ 
図表４の通り、トランプ政権発足後のタームプレミアムは、経済
政策不確実性指数と概ね連動してきた。関税、移民、拡張財政、
中銀への政治圧力、地政学リスクなど、トランプ政権における政
策の予見可能性の低下が、ドル資産への信認を損ねたことが
背景である。  
しかし足元では両者の関係に変化がみられる。これは市場の関
心がトランプリスクから、財政悪化や国債需給懸念へ移行した
可能性を示唆するものだ。

図表４：タームプレミアムはトランプリスクと共に動いてきた

注：経済政策不確実性指数は、政治・経済、財政・金融政策、外交、安保、地政学等
の先行き不透明感を表す指数  
出所：EPU、NY連銀、Bloomberg、ソニーFG

財政規律の緩みと「トランプ配当」 
もっとも、財政懸念は今に始まった話ではない。図表５は米財政
赤字のＧＤＰ比と失業率（上下反転）の関係を示したものである。
かつては、失業率が悪化すれば財政のアクセルを踏み、改善
すればブレーキを踏むという一定の規律があった。しかし、トラ
ンプ政権第一期が始動した 2017 年頃から、完全雇用にもかか
わらず財政赤字が拡大しており、放漫財政が常態化している。  
では、なぜ今になって財政問題に焦点が当たるのか。背景には、
中間選挙が迫るなかで加速するトランプ政権のポピュリズム政
策がある。政権支持率はインフレを理由に過去最低水準に落ち
込んでいる。そこでトランプ大統領は、共和党が中間選挙で上
下両院を制した場合、有権者一人当たり5,000ドルの「トランプ配
当」を支給する方針を打ち出した。1.2 兆ドル規模の歳出拡大の
財源をどう賄うのか、それが財政収支と国債需給にどう影響す
るのかが不安視されている。

図表５：トランプ第一期政権以降、財政規律が緩み、 
好況下での財政赤字拡大が常態化

出所：BLS、BEA、ソニーFG

これは悪循環の入口である。インフレによる国民の不満に財政
政策で応じれば、需要は過熱し、インフレが加速する。そのイン
フレが新たな不満を生み、さらなる財政出動を招けば国債発行
が増え、長期金利が上昇する。また、利払い費の増加は財政赤
字をさらに拡大させる。結果として、インフレ、財政不安、長期金
利上昇が相互に増幅し合う負の連鎖に陥りかねない。最終的に
財政規律を迫るのは債券市場となり、金利上昇が経済・金融市
場に深刻な打撃を与えて初めて財政再建が進む展開も十分に
想定される。

国債市場の問題は「供給量」より「吸収力」 
現在、市場の焦点は、国債発行額の増加よりも、その吸収力（国
債需要）の低下に移りつつある。要因は４つある。

第１に、ＦＲＢの資産圧縮の進展である。これは2022 年以降の
動きだが、ウォーシュＦＲＢ議長がＱＴに前向きであることで、あ
らためて警戒を呼んでいる。  
第２に、ウクライナ戦争後の傾向として、新興国を中心に「ドル離
れ」が進み、各国の中央銀行が外貨準備として保有する米国債
を売却して金の準備を積み増す動きが続いている。  
第３に、ＡＩ関連企業の大量起債がある。データセンターなどの設
備投資の資金を得るため、大規模な社債発行が行われており、
資本の争奪戦を招いている。  
第４に、中東情勢に伴う原油高がインフレやインフレ期待を高め、
債券投資家を慎重にさせている。

特に第１の要因は次頁図表６に明瞭に表れている。ＦＲＢの国債
保有比率とタームプレミアムには逆相関がみられ、ＦＲＢが資産
圧縮を進めるほど、国債を引き受ける民間投資家がより高い上
乗せ金利を要求する姿である。今後ＱＴが加速すれば、ターム
プレミアムにはさらなる上昇圧力がかかるだろう。

（サプライズ指数）
（年/月）
期待政策金利
（市場の政策金利予想、右軸）
エコノミックサプライズ指数
（雇用・物価関連、30日先行、左軸）
（期待政策金利、％）
イラン攻撃
図表６：ＦＲＢの資産圧縮がタームプレミアムを押し上げ

注：米国債保有比率＝FRBの米国債保有額／米国債残高  
出所：Bloomberg、ソニーFG

ベッセント財務長官は長期債のバイバック拡充で流動性の改善
を図っているが、効果は限定的だ。バイバックは市場機能を支
えても、財政赤字や国債残高を減らさないためである。財政や
需給の根本的な改善が見込めない以上、当面のタームプレミア
ムは 0.6～0.9％の高水準で推移しやすい。  
なお、各種世論調査では中間選挙で共和党が上下両院を失うと
の見方が高まっている。その場合、①新議会発足前にトランプ
政権が対イラン攻撃を強化する可能性、②新議会・民主党が主
導する財政拡張の加速、③関税や反移民政策など大統領令に
よる政策の一段の先鋭化、④ＦＲＢへの政治圧力の強化などが
想定される。インフレや財政悪化、中銀の独立性への懸念を高
め、タームプレミアムの押し上げにつながる可能性がある。

期待政策金利は利上げを織り込み上昇 
一方、期待政策金利は経済指標の強弱に敏感に反応している。
図表７のエコノミック・サプライズ指数は経済指標の予想と実績
の差を示すが、イラン攻撃以降はインフレ指標の上振れによっ
て指数が上昇し、それとともに期待政策金利も切り上がった。ま
た、直近では９月ＰＭＩの予想以上の強さも利上げ期待を高めて
いる。

図表７：期待政策金利は経済指標の強弱に反応

注：サプライズ指数は経済指標の市場予想と実績の乖離。ここでは雇用と物価に関
するサプライズ指数の合計値を用いた  
出所：Bloomberg、ソニーFG 
期待政策金利は先々の政策金利の予想値であるため、市場の
利上げ観測に沿って動く。図表８の通り、市場は年内２回以上の
利上げを織り込んでおり、数年後の政策金利は５％を突破する
との見立てである。一方、市場の予想は先走りしやすく、大きな
揺り戻しも生じやすい。そうした点を考慮すると、向こう数年の期
待政策金利は3.9％～4.6％での推移となろう。

図表８：期待政策金利は将来の政策金利を織り込む

出所：NY連銀、Bloomberg、ソニーFG

以上を総合すると、長期金利＝期待政策金利（3.9～4.6％）＋タ
ームプレミアム（0.6～0.9％）となり、当面は4.5～5.5％のレンジで
の推移が見込まれる。ただし、冒頭で見たように、現在の長期
金利はすでに米国経済・株式市場に打撃を与え得る水準に達し
ている。原油高や国債需給懸念などにより、景気拡大を背景とし
ない「悪い金利上昇」がさらに進めば、景気悪化と株安が顕在化
するリスクは一段と高まろう。

超長期金利が映す財政不安 
図表９の通り、米国の30 年金利と10 年金利は密に連動してい
るが、両者のスプレッドは不況期に拡大し、好況期に縮小する
傾向がある。不況期は将来の利下げ観測から短中期ゾーンの
金利が大きく低下する一方、超長期ゾーンは動きにくいためだ。

図表９：米30 年-10 年金利は好況期に一致、不況期に乖離

出所：Bloomberg、ソニーFG

（％） （％）
ところがトランプ政権発足以降（2025 年～）は、堅調な景気が続
いたにもかかわらずスプレッドが拡大しており、景気循環では説
明できない。財政赤字と国債需給への懸念が、30 年金利に上乗
せされているとみるべきである。

図表10 は30 年-10 年金利スプレッドと景気（ＧＤＰギャップ）の関
係を見たものである。過去は両者が密接に連動しており、超長
期ゾーンのイールドカーブの傾きは景気次第で動いていたこと
が改めて確認できる。しかし足元ではこの関係に明確な乖離が
生じており、景気では説明できない上乗せ分＝財政プレミアム
の存在を裏づけている。

図表10：ＧＤＰギャップと30 年－10 年金利スプレッド

注：GDPギャップの予想は米議会予算局による 
出所：米議会予算局、Bloomberg、ソニーFG

ＧＤＰギャップから見れば妥当なスプレッドは0.2％ｐｔ程度だが、
一時はこれが0.7％ｐｔまで拡大し、足元でも 0.4％ｐｔ程度となって
いる。当面はこの財政プレミアムが残り、30 年金利は10 年金利
＋0.4％ｐｔ程度で推移すると見込まれる。10 年金利の予想レンジ
に基づけば、30 年金利は4.9～5.9％となる。中間選挙に向けて
財政不安が強まれば、景気が減速しても超長期金利が上昇し続
けるリスクがある。

米国の財政問題は日本の金利に波及 
米国の超長期金利の上昇は日本にも波及する。図表 11 の通り、
日本の40 年金利は、日本の10 年金利に内包される「国内要因」
と、米国30 年金利に反映される「海外要因」の双方によって押し
上げられてきた。  
国内要因としては、日銀の独立性を巡るビハインド・ザ・カーブ
懸念や、高市政権の積極財政に対する信認問題が金利上昇要
因となってきた。ただし、７月末の日米協調介入以降は、米国か
ら政策修正を求める圧力が強まった。８月末のＧ20 ではベッセ
ント財務長官から「リフレ政策をやめるべき」との要求もあった。
こうした外圧は、日銀の利上げを後押しする一方、高市政権の
財政拡大路線には一定の歯止めとなろう。

図表11： 40 年金利は国内要因と海外要因が押し上げ

出所：Bloomberg、ソニーFG

今後、日銀の利上げペースが加速する可能性はあるが、長期
金利の水準を左右するのは利上げの最終到達点である。市場
が予想するターミナルレート（OIS２年先１か月）は既に日銀の中
立金利レンジ（1.1～2.5％）の上限を突破していることから、期待
政策金利の上昇余地は大きくないと思われる。このため、国内
要因による超長期金利への上昇圧力は徐々に弱まっていくと考
えられる（高市政権の政策スタンスの先鋭化や日銀が中立金利
を上回る引き締めを行うなどの不確実性は残る）。  
一方、海外要因の影響は強まりそうだ。米30年金利が本稿の予
想通り4.9～5.9％で推移すれば、日本の40 年金利は3.8～4.9％
程度で推移する可能性が高い（※）。もっとも、このレンジは米30
年金利のみを説明変数とする推計に基づくため、日本の10 年
金利に反映される国内要因によって上下に振れる余地がある。  
現在、日本の超長期金利を左右しているのは、国内の金融・財
政政策と米国の財政・国債需給だが、重点は後者へ移りつつあ
る。今後は日銀や国内景気に加えて、米国の30 年債入札やＱＴ、
財政政策が日本の超長期債市場を占う上で重要性を増すので
はないか。

渡辺 浩志 
※）米 30 年金利と日本の 40 年金利の間の直接的な裁定関係は薄いが、両市 
場はグローバル投資家による相対価値取引やポートフォリオ調整を通じて 
連動している。

1
《製藥新藥研發有斬獲 ，減重藥引領製藥成長動能 》
近月製藥行業迎來兩 個重大發現 ，美國默克跟莫德
納宣布，雙方合作開發的個人化 mRNA 黑色素瘤疫
苗搭配免疫療法藥物 Keytruda，在第三期臨床試驗
中取得重大成功 ，市場期待適應症拓展到其他領域 ，
然仍待臨床實驗結果 。美國FDA核准了可以提高存
活率的胰臟癌藥物 ，顯示製藥行業新藥研發動能持

續。但同時也將迎來第三輪藥價談判在 10月將公布
降價幅度，今年也是第一輪藥價談判正式實施年度 ，
但影響並不顯著 ，主因種類少 、標價折扣與淨價的
差距較低 、整體批發藥價仍上漲 ，多數製藥業因抗
肥胖、腫瘤藥、免疫等核心新藥銷售成長 ，藥廠展
望仍佳，其中減重藥仍是主要引領市場成長動能 ，
預期自費及國際市場均為成長動力 ，且受醫療保險
過渡計畫，使保險人得以報銷GLP-1用藥，雖早期
資料來源：JPMorgan，2026/8/28，中國信託商業銀行整理，

重點摘要
• 醫療器材在心血管 、電生理學
領域等必要性手術需求仍佳 ，
下半年有其他新產品出貨 ，雖

術需求放緩持續。
• 管理式醫療在政策方面多空兼

推廣較慢 ，但釋放市場將使抗肥胖藥銷售成長增強 。
《必要性手術需求仍佳，可延期性手術需求放緩》
受成長市場趨於成熟 ，領導廠商因新競爭者加入競
爭、個別公司部分產品召回或產品責任賠償判決風
險，壓低醫療器材評價面 ，但近期也因市場廣度增
加，修復估值。雖醫療科技廠商2Q26財報展現必要
的醫療手術需求健康，嬌生、亞培的電生理(如脈衝

電場消融PFA)滲透率增加 ，心血管類手術下半年仍
成長，其他新產品推出；亞培糖尿病器材需求因新
產品出貨增加 。但因自付成本增加 ，擇期手術和外
科手術需求疲軟延伸至年底 ，使機器人手術成長率
區間接近 14.5%，下半年低於上半年 ，將以擴大翻
新系統的布署降低門檻及增加門診手術需求 ，擴大
目標市場。醫療器材基本面喜憂參半。
《政策多空兼具 ，但管理式醫療利潤優先於市佔率 》
管理式醫療面臨政策多方影響如受到 ACA交易所增
強型聯邦保費補貼到期失效退保 、340B計畫允許醫
療院所以折扣價採購門診藥品 ，但管理式醫療機構
難以分辨差異的損失，利好之處在2027年聯邦醫療
保險優勢計畫 (MA) 最 終 費 率 高 於 預 期 ， 但
Medicaid體系面臨聯邦預算削減與資格緊縮的壓力 。
從2Q26財報展現業者利潤優先於市佔率，重新奪回
定價主導 ，UNH的聯邦醫療保險優值計畫 (MA)利
潤超預期復甦 ，下半年更主動砍掉部分不賺錢的
MA會員人數 ，將拉升利潤率 ，並將透過保單調整

及定價，維持業績穩定 ，雖商業險的醫療成本趨勢
依然居高不下使下半年利潤率回升受阻 ，但因運用
AI降低住院率 、優化工作流程 、提升營運效率及客
群服務滿意度 ，對下半年醫療成本趨勢雖因週期因
素走升，但增速低於預期 ，因產業特性獲利多數於
上半年實現 ，雖下半年獲利低於上半年 ，但仍較去
年同期成長。

宏观点评 
 【粤开宏观】对解决地方财政问题的
思考：兼谈地方附加税的意义 
2026 年 09 月 21 日  投资要点

近期报告  
《 【粤开宏观】 央企清欠政策的传导逻辑及
长效防控机制》2026-09-21 
《 【粤开宏观】中美K 型分化的本质与区
别》2026-09-20 
《 【粤开宏观】美国K 型分化下的加息逻
辑 —— 美 联 储 9 月 议 息 会 议 点 评 》
2026-09-17 
《 【粤开宏观】8 月经济运行图景： 工业与
服务业分化，房地产呈现三重分化》
2026-09-15 
《 【粤开宏观】城投转型 的 进展与困难
——2025 年 城 投 年 报 观 察 （ 二 ） 》
2026-09-13

一、征求意见稿的三个特点及需要关注的两个问题 
此次改革的内容呈现出三个特点： 
一是税收（收费）制度基本平移，整体税负维持稳定；二是赋予地方一定的
自主管理权限，增强地方财力自主性；三是注重与之前税费征收制度和相关
法律的衔接。 
总体上看，由于地方附加税主要是对原来一税两费的合并以及总体平移，更
多是规范税收、简化征管并赋予地方更多管理权限，对宏观税负以及微观主
体负担影响相对有限。当然，实施落地还需要进一步明确或评估两个问题：
一是合理评估对相关纳税人税费负担的影响，尤其是对当前正在享受相关税
收优惠政策的微观主体的影响；二是统筹考虑相关税费收入的使用，原“一税
两费”理论上是专款专用，未来需要统筹考虑合并后对原来支出领域的影响
等。 
二、解决地方财政困难需要从中央和地方同时发力，需要改革财税制度 
对地方政府而言：从短期看要加大资产盘活力度，盘活资产靠的是一系列制
度支持，例如资产清查、资产产权瑕疵修复、资产处置尽职免责、国资考核
激励机制等，缺乏制度支持的资产盘活将沦为方式单一且不可持续的粗放资
产处置。从长期看，地方政府要推动城投转型并重塑税基，切实因地制宜找
到符合自身优势的特色产业，要打造良好的营商环境而非一味依赖补贴和税
收优惠洼地来吸引产业。 
对中央政府而言：从短期看有必要加大对地方的转移支付、增加均衡性转移
支付的比重，增强地方政府获得的转移支付的规模以及可支配程度；持续增
加转移支付不是长期之道，但短期是非常有必要的，有助于地方政府度过房
地产持续调整、化债重压、新旧动能转型的关键阶段。从中长期看，有必要
通过财税制度改革来稳定宏观税负、完善央地事权和支出责任划分、改革政
府投融资体制机制，具体可通过以下改革实现。 
一是逐步稳定宏观税负，扭转宏观税负持续下行的趋势；二是推动财税体制
改革，厘清政府与市场关系，科学界定政府职责与规模，约束地方政府规模
持续扩张；三是探索建立债务与资本预算，加强地方政府债务管理，降低地
方对债务的依赖；四是深化地方政府投融资体制改革，根据项目的收益特征
和投资周期匹配相应的资金来源，形成国债、地方一般债、地方专项债、城
投债各司其职的格局。 
风险提示：财税体制改革举措超预期、外部冲击超预期

### Page 3

宏观点评
请务必阅读最后特别声明与免责条款 www.ykzq.com  3 / 7
近年来随着中国经济新旧动能加快转换以及房地产持续调整，宏观税负总体呈下行
态势，同时地方财政收入尤其是综合财力受冲击明显，财政紧平衡已成为常态。在此背
景下，如何稳定宏观税负、如何解决地方财政收支矛盾，事关地方政府积极性的发挥，
事关积极财政政策更好落地，事关经济平稳运行，已成为各方关注的焦点问题，亟需破
局。三中全会和四中全会对此做了方向性、指导性的部署安排，要求 “增加地方自主财
力”“规范税收优惠”“研究探索同新业态相适应的税收制度”。
为贯彻落实 2024 年 7 月三中全会提出的“研究把城市维护建设税、教育费附加、地
方教育附加合并为地方附加税”，2026 年 8 月 28 日，财政部和国家税务总局公布了《中
华人民共和国地方附加税法（征求意见稿） 》 （下称“征求意见稿”） 。这是落实重要部署的
关键举措，不仅标志着我国税收法定和税收制度改革又取得新的进展，还是增强地方财
政收入稳定性的重要改革措施。总体上看，地方附加税短期对宏观税负和地方财力的影

响相对有限， 更主要是简化税费制度、 优化税费结构、 增加财力自主性、 推进税收法定。
从根本上解决地方财政问题，除了推出地方附加税外，还要从规范税收优惠、改革税收
制度 （税制跟上数字经济服务经济和人工智能经济发展步伐） 、 盘活地方国有资产、上移
事权和支出责任、优化转移支付制度等方面共同施策。
一、征求意见稿的三个特点及需要关注的两个问题
此次改革的内容呈现出三个特点：
一是税收（收费）制度基本平移，整体税负维持稳定。地方附加税是将我国施行已
久的“一税两费”——即城市维护建设税 （1985 年开征） 、 教育费附加 （1986 年开始征收） 、
地方教育附加（2011 年开始征收）——合并为一个税种，而非开征新的税种。这样做，
不仅明显简化了税费征收， 而且使之前“两费”正式转为具有基本税收法律征收依据的“税”
的形式，增强了征收的法定性和稳定性。税率设置上充分考虑了税制简化、税负稳定、
地方差异等因素，采用浮动比例税率，税费负担保持基本稳定。2025 年，城市维护建设
税、教育费附加、地方教育附加的规模分别为 5170 亿元、2382 亿元、1582 亿元，合计

费附加按照单位和个人实际缴纳的增值税、 消费税税额的3%征收， 地方教育附加按照单
位和个人实际缴纳的增值税、消费税税额的 2%征收。 《征求意见稿》赋予了各省在综合
统筹考虑本地区经济社会发展和促进全国统一大市场建设等情况的前提下，在 11%—13%
幅度内自行确定省内适用税率的权利，是增强地方自主财力的重要举措。
三是注重与之前税费征收制度和相关法律的衔接。例如，规定了国务院可以根据国
民经济和社会发展的需要，对于特殊产业和群体以及重大突发事件应对等情形，减征或
者免征地方附加税，保留了税收优惠窗口；明确了征收的基本要素，与之前的征管制度
有效衔接等。
总体上看，由于地方附加税主要是对原来一税两费的合并以及总体平移，更多是规
范税收、简化征管并赋予地方更多管理权限，对宏观税负以及微观主体负担影响相对有
限。当然，实施落地还需要进一步明确或评估两个问题：一是合理评估对相关纳税人税
费负担的影响，尤其是对当前正在享受相关税收优惠政策的微观主体的影响；二是统筹
考虑相关税费收入的使用，原“一税两费”理论上是专款专用，未来需要统筹考虑合并后

对原来支出领域的影响等。

### Page 4

宏观点评

二、解决地方财政困难需要从中央和地方同时发力， 需要改革
财税制度
对地方政府而言： 从短期看要加大资产盘活力度， 盘活资产靠的是一系列制度支持，
例如资产清查、资产产权瑕疵修复、资产处置尽职免责、国资考核激励机制等，缺乏制
度支持的资产盘活将沦为方式单一且不可持续的粗放资产处置。从长期看，地方政府要
推动城投转型并重塑税基，切实因地制宜找到符合自身优势的特色产业，要打造良好的
营商环境而非一味依赖补贴和税收优惠洼地来吸引产业。
对中央政府而言：从短期看有必要加大对地方的转移支付、增加均衡性转移支付的
比重，增强地方政府获得的转移支付的规模以及可支配程度；持续增加转移支付不是长
期之道，但短期是非常有必要的，有助于地方政府度过房地产持续调整、化债重压、新
旧动能转型的关键阶段。从中长期看，有必要通过财税制度改革来稳定宏观税负、完善
央地事权和支出责任划分、改革政府投融资体制机制，具体可通过以下改革实现。
一是逐步稳定宏观税负，扭转宏观税负持续下行的趋势。其一，积极财政政策实施

方式有必要从侧重收入端减税降费转向支出端扩大支出为主，对新增减税降费要慎之又
慎，避免税收优惠泛化。其二，规范税收优惠，清理地方政府违规出台的税收政策。前
期优惠政策自然到期总体采取不再延续的办法，确有需要另行研究。税收优惠要考虑产
业生命周期和经济周期，产业生命周期从幼稚期走向成熟期，税收优惠要逐步退坡，避
免助涨“内卷式竞争”；重大内外部冲击时期出台的阶段性优惠政策不能固化沉淀，待相
关因素消失后要及时取消相关政策。其三，优化调整当前对普通老百姓影响不大但有助
于促进共同富裕、促进绿色发展的税种。例如，推动消费税扩围，推进消费税扩围至高
污染、 高耗能消费品以及高端服务业； 对超高净值群体的海外收入和财富强化税收征管，
研究遗产税赠与税；推进资源税和环保税税率适度提高，减少资源能源浪费，维护国家
能源安全；推进油电同权同税负，对于新能源汽车对道路的损坏征收相应税收。其四，
研究数字经济、人工智能时代下的数字资产税和超额利润调节税等。AI 时代加剧“供强
需弱”，财富与收入分配更加极化，有必要研究“超额利润调节税”。

二是推动财税体制改革，厘清政府与市场关系，科学界定政府职责与规模，约束地
方政府规模持续扩张。其一，厘清政府与市场关系，科学界定政府规模。要深化行政事
业单位改革， 强化绩效管理， 收缩政府边界， 精兵简政， 切实减少财政供养人口。 其二，
深化中央与地方财政体制改革，减少对地方政府尤其是基层政府的压力，尤其要帮助地
方政府避免成为无限责任政府。逐步上收事权和支出责任至中央政府，减轻地方政府支
出责任和财政压力，逐步做实公共安全、食品药品监管、养老保险等事权的垂直管理和
部门实体化。其三，推动公用事业价格改革，避免持续大规模的低效财政补贴对财政的
压力，改暗补为明补。
三是探索建立债务与资本预算，加强地方政府债务管理，降低地方对债务的依赖。
其一，尽快摸清长期大量的专项债投资形成的资产类别、质量情况、现金流情况，尽快
分门别类理顺理清分属在财政、国资、机关事务管理局等不同部门的资产。其二，建立
债务与资本预算，明确债务用到哪里去、形成哪些资产、如何偿还等，形成制度约束。
债务预算是对政府债务规模、结构、还本付息计划等进行详细规划的预算，是现行债务

限额管理的升级版。资本预算即针对政府固定资产投资等能够形成公共固定资产的财政
支出活动编制的预算。尤其是专项债债务形成的资产具有一定收益或潜在收益，需要积
极提高相关资产收益率，避免资产资源浪费。其三，完善考核制度约束体系，严防新增
隐性债务。完善政绩考核体系，降低 GDP 增速考核权重，增加债务风险防控指标。
四是深化地方政府投融资体制改革，根据项目的收益特征和投资周期匹配相应的资

### Page 5

宏观点评
请务必阅读最后特别声明与免责条款 www.ykzq.com  5 / 7
金来源，形成国债、地方一般债、地方专项债、城投债各司其职的格局。其一，对于纯
公益性项目，使用财政资金予以保障，其中全国性、跨区域项目由国债保障，对于地方
无收益的项目由地方一般债保障、对于地方有一定收益的项目由地方专项债保障（鉴于
合格专项债项目越来越少，该部分从长期看宜控制新增规模） 。 其二，城投企业原则上不
再承担纯公益性项目建设，重点参与具有一定经营收益但社会资本参与意愿不足的经营
性项目，采取市场化融资方式，从源头减少政府隐性债务形成。对于城投参与的有一定
经营收入但收益偏低的准公益性项目，要完善参与机制，包括明确政府出资比例与偿债
责任、提供投资补助或运营补贴资金的方式等问题。

股票投资评级说明 
投资评级分为股票投资评级和行业投资评级。

股票投资评级标准 
报告发布日后的 12 个月内公司股价的涨跌幅度相对同期沪深 300 指数的涨跌幅为基准，投资建议的评级标准为： 
买入：相对大盘涨幅大于 10%； 
增持：相对大盘涨幅在 5%~10%之间； 
持有：相对大盘涨幅在-5%~5%之间； 
减持：相对大盘涨幅小于-5%。

行业投资评级标准 
报告发布日后的 12 个月内行业股票指数的涨跌幅度相对同期沪深 300 指数的涨跌幅为基准，投资建议的评级标准为： 
增持：我们预计未来报告期内，行业整体回报高于基准指数 5%以上； 
中性：我们预计未来报告期内，行业整体回报介于基准指数-5%与 5%之间； 
减持：我们预计未来报告期内，行业整体回报低于基准指数 5%以下。
